Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mult_add_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
- output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `scanin` = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: `ay` is driven directly, and `scanout` is left unconnected); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, dla_like.small.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified dla_like.small under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.81328 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 114 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 1.67592e+08 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 911396, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.04068e+15. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in dsp_block_16_8_false: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then one adder chain of 12 carry stages, then one LUT, into a flip-flop with synchronous clear (resulta_tmp); wire 1.73 ns into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder, 1.39 into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in a different dsp_block_16_8_false instance (endpoint group $sdff~5296, not path 1's $sdff~4939), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, one adder chain of 15 carry stages, one LUT, into the resulta_tmp flip-flop, 6.808 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: dla_like.small.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: resulta in dsp_block_16_8_false (the 24-bit sum ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin, line 11745)
BLOCKS: 144
TILES: 194
STAGE_NS: 3.36
MAPPING: per dsp_block_16_8_false instance, two blocks, neither chained.
Block A (region $1166): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, ax[15:0]} (module port ax), ay={11'b0, ay[7:0]} (module port ay), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_a[63:0] (only res_a[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Block B (region $1167): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, bx[15:0]} (module port bx), ay={11'b0, by[7:0]} (module port by), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_b[63:0] (only res_b[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Soft logic kept in the module: cin_reg (24 bits, synchronous clear on aclr, else chainin[23:0]), assign resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg; (24-bit context, wraps mod 2^24), assign chainout = {40'b0, resulta};. ena stays unread, as in the original.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted and why

Both candidate products of dsp_block_16_8_false are converted, all 72 instances of each: $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166 (ax_reg*ay_reg) and $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167 (bx_reg*by_reg). They are the two products of the module's one sum (line 11745), and that sum's stage is the design's slowest (6.81328 ns) and second slowest (6.808 ns); the two reported paths are two different instances of the same shape, so the period only moves if every instance of the shape is fixed. 144 blocks, 2 per module instance.

The whole rewrite lives inside module dsp_block_16_8_false (lines 11696-11752). Its port list, every instantiation (line 11679 in dot_product_16_8_30_2) and every other module stay untouched; the reference testbench drives this module's ports (ena=1, chainin=0) and compares resulta and chainout every cycle, so the module's 2-cycle port-to-resulta latency and its aclr semantics must be preserved exactly, and they are (see 4).

  1. Candidates left, and why

All sixteen winograd rows are left: winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...:8966$733(sum_in=winograd_transform_0)#1..#4, the same four for winograd_transform_1, and the eight corresponding rows of $mul$...:8967$734. One reason covers all of them, and each of the three below is sufficient on its own:

  • Truncation. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16 bits (lines 8913, 8966-8967), so each product is reduced mod 2^16 before it enters the 20-bit adder tree in winograd_adder_16_20_4 (lines 8879-8895). A block carries the full 37-bit product into its sum, and sum(p_i mod 2^16) != (sum p_i) mod 2^20. Reproducing the per-product truncation requires the product outside the block, which defeats the conversion.
  • Visibility. Each dsp_out_* is read by several consumers outside any one sum: e.g. dsp_out_1_5 feeds winograd_adder_16_20_4_WA10.data0x (line 9833), is negated into f5 (line 9853) and shifted into f15 (line 9886); dsp_out_1_4 feeds WA00, WA10, WA20 and WA30 (lines 9810, 9843, 9872, 9902). A value read elsewhere cannot go inside a block.
  • Sign. coefa/coefb hold negative constants (-5, -10, -20, -25, lines 8928-8964) as 16-bit two's complement; the block zero-extends ay to 19 bits and its result is unsigned, so only the low 16 bits of the product would agree, and the tree adds 20-bit values. The same holds for the negated and arithmetically shifted operands.

Additionally, those sums mix the products with many non-product terms (shifted feature_reg_*) in a 4-level pipelined tree; a block chain would also change their latency.

  1. Blocks, tiles, and the longest series of blocks in a stage
  • Blocks added: 144 (2 per dsp_block_16_8_false instance x 72 instances). A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the converted logic occupies 144 tiles.
  • Baseline accounting: the design has 256 multiplies on DSPs (72 x 2 in dsp_block_16_8_false, 56 x 2 in winograd_dsp_16), i.e. 128 two_mult_18x19 blocks packed into 114 tiles (100 tiles with one block, 14 with two, = 256 multiplies). After the rewrite the 144 converted multiplies leave that pool and the 56 winograd blocks remain; at the baseline's packing density they take about 50 tiles. Total 194 tiles (worst case 200 if none of them pair), out of 306 on the device.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. Nothing is chained: chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block and every chainout is left unconnected, because a chained hop costs an extra register stage (block A's chainout is 1 cycle after its operands, and block B registers chainin at its entry, giving 3 cycles from port to result) while the module's contract is 2 cycles port-to-resulta. With no series of blocks, the block-to-block wire never appears; the block's own stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the 6.81328 ns baseline stage.
  1. Correctness

Original (line 11745, chainin tied to 0 at the only instantiation, line 11687): operand registers cleared by aclr, then resulta_tmp <= (ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin)[23:0], also cleared by aclr; chainout = {40'b0, resulta_tmp}.

After: block A's input register reproduces ax_reg/ay_reg and block B's reproduces bx_reg/by_reg (same clock, same synchronous active-high clear driven by aclr, no enable in the original either). Block A's result register holds p0 = ax*ay exactly (16x8 unsigned, max 16711425 < 2^24, so res_a[23:0] is the exact product; same for p1), both cleared by aclr on the same edge that cleared resulta_tmp. cin_reg reproduces the one-cycle-later arrival of chainin at the old result register, so no assumption about chainin is needed; since the single instantiation ties it to 0, it constant-folds away in synthesis. resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg in 24-bit context equals (p0 + p1 + chainin) mod 2^24 on the same cycle as before, and chainout keeps its definition. Under clear, all three summands are 0, so resulta is 0, exactly as the cleared resulta_tmp. Every term of the original is kept; nothing inside a block is read elsewhere (the products were read only by that adder).

Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first aclr; the design holds aclr (reset_3, line 11343, from ~(i_valid || valid_11)) high while not valid, and the reference testbench asserts reset for 4 cycles and compares only after release, so nothing relies on an initial value.

Editor notes: delete the now-dead ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and their always block, keep the port list and the unused ena/mode as they are, and instantiate mult_add_int_18x19 by name only (do not add its definition to dla_like.small.v).

  1. Register stages, before and after (both converted regions)
  • Before: module port -> soft operand register (stage 1) -> hard multiply, 12-15-stage soft carry chain, one LUT -> resulta_tmp soft flip-flop with synchronous clear (stage 2) -> resulta. 2 register stages; the products are registered 0 stages before the sum (the table's "0 stage(s)").
  • After: module port -> block input register (stage 1) -> hard multiply inside the block -> block result register (stage 2) -> soft 24-bit adder, combinational -> resulta. Still 2 register stages, same latency, same cycle-by-cycle values; the products are now registered 1 stage before the sum, and the sum is combinational behind that register.
  1. Stages, and what leaves which stage
  • Leaving the slowest stage: the 24-bit soft adder chain and the final LUT leave the multiply's stage. The baseline stage was [soft operand FF -> wire 1.73 -> hard multiply -> wire 0.96 -> 12 carry stages -> wire 1.39 -> LUT -> resulta_tmp] = 6.81328 ns, the design's slowest; the second slowest, 6.808 ns, is the same shape in another instance and is converted too. The 48 soft operand flip-flops and the 24 resulta_tmp flip-flops per instance (72 x 72 = 5184 flip-flops in all) move into the blocks' registers and disappear from soft logic.
  • The multiply is now alone between the block's two registers: 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • The new longest stage the rewrite leaves is the one the adder moved into: block result register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire block into soft logic 1.376 (worst on this design; median 0.960) + 24-bit carry chain and LUT about 0.51 (what the baseline path leaves for its 12 carry stages plus LUT) + wire 1.39 to that LUT + setup 0.019 = 3.36 ns; with the median wire it is about 2.94 ns.
  • The stage feeding the blocks is register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire register to first block up to 2.461 (median 1.835) + input-register setup 0.019 = up to 2.54 ns, shorter than 3.36.
  • So the rewrite's own slowest stage is 3.36 ns against a 6.81328 ns baseline. The design's remaining stages are untouched and unlengthened; all of them are below the two converted paths, but the seed-1 report does not expose the third path, so the achieved period is max(3.36, slowest untouched stage).
  • Expected objective: tiles 114 -> 194 is +2.03e7 area, against about 1.45e7 recovered from the 5184 removed flip-flops, so area goes to roughly 1.73e8 (about +3.5%); delay drops by about half; wirelength is roughly flat to +15% (the same operand and result buses, no block-to-block routing, more spread over DSP columns). Product well below 1.04068e15.

DESIGN_CHANGE: inside dsp_block_16_8_false, registering the two products instead of the operands (ports straight into the multiplies, a product register, then the adder) would split the same 6.81 ns stage with no extra DSP tiles, at about 4.9 ns per stage (soft FF -> wire 1.73 -> multiply 2.14 -> wire 0.96 -> product FF); it keeps the module's 2-cycle latency and is a change to the design rather than a rewrite onto the block.
DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 carried its products at full width (and winograd_adder_16_20_4 summed them at that width) instead of truncating to the 16-bit resa_reg/resb_reg, and if the shared products were not re-read, negated and shifted outside their sums, the winograd trees could be folded into chains of these blocks.

output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The design source is dla_like.small.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted and why

Both candidate products of dsp_block_16_8_false are converted, all 72 instances of each: $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166 (ax_reg*ay_reg) and $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167 (bx_reg*by_reg). They are the two products of the module's one sum (line 11745), and that sum's stage is the design's slowest (6.81328 ns) and second slowest (6.808 ns); the two reported paths are two different instances of the same shape, so the period only moves if every instance of the shape is fixed. 144 blocks, 2 per module instance.

The whole rewrite lives inside module dsp_block_16_8_false (lines 11696-11752). Its port list, every instantiation (line 11679 in dot_product_16_8_30_2) and every other module stay untouched; the reference testbench drives this module's ports (ena=1, chainin=0) and compares resulta and chainout every cycle, so the module's 2-cycle port-to-resulta latency and its aclr semantics must be preserved exactly, and they are (see 4).

  1. Candidates left, and why

All sixteen winograd rows are left: winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...:8966$733(sum_in=winograd_transform_0)#1..#4, the same four for winograd_transform_1, and the eight corresponding rows of $mul$...:8967$734. One reason covers all of them, and each of the three below is sufficient on its own:

  • Truncation. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16 bits (lines 8913, 8966-8967), so each product is reduced mod 2^16 before it enters the 20-bit adder tree in winograd_adder_16_20_4 (lines 8879-8895). A block carries the full 37-bit product into its sum, and sum(p_i mod 2^16) != (sum p_i) mod 2^20. Reproducing the per-product truncation requires the product outside the block, which defeats the conversion.
  • Visibility. Each dsp_out_* is read by several consumers outside any one sum: e.g. dsp_out_1_5 feeds winograd_adder_16_20_4_WA10.data0x (line 9833), is negated into f5 (line 9853) and shifted into f15 (line 9886); dsp_out_1_4 feeds WA00, WA10, WA20 and WA30 (lines 9810, 9843, 9872, 9902). A value read elsewhere cannot go inside a block.
  • Sign. coefa/coefb hold negative constants (-5, -10, -20, -25, lines 8928-8964) as 16-bit two's complement; the block zero-extends ay to 19 bits and its result is unsigned, so only the low 16 bits of the product would agree, and the tree adds 20-bit values. The same holds for the negated and arithmetically shifted operands.

Additionally, those sums mix the products with many non-product terms (shifted feature_reg_*) in a 4-level pipelined tree; a block chain would also change their latency.

  1. Blocks, tiles, and the longest series of blocks in a stage
  • Blocks added: 144 (2 per dsp_block_16_8_false instance x 72 instances). A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the converted logic occupies 144 tiles.
  • Baseline accounting: the design has 256 multiplies on DSPs (72 x 2 in dsp_block_16_8_false, 56 x 2 in winograd_dsp_16), i.e. 128 two_mult_18x19 blocks packed into 114 tiles (100 tiles with one block, 14 with two, = 256 multiplies). After the rewrite the 144 converted multiplies leave that pool and the 56 winograd blocks remain; at the baseline's packing density they take about 50 tiles. Total 194 tiles (worst case 200 if none of them pair), out of 306 on the device.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. Nothing is chained: chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block and every chainout is left unconnected, because a chained hop costs an extra register stage (block A's chainout is 1 cycle after its operands, and block B registers chainin at its entry, giving 3 cycles from port to result) while the module's contract is 2 cycles port-to-resulta. With no series of blocks, the block-to-block wire never appears; the block's own stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the 6.81328 ns baseline stage.
  1. Correctness

Original (line 11745, chainin tied to 0 at the only instantiation, line 11687): operand registers cleared by aclr, then resulta_tmp <= (ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin)[23:0], also cleared by aclr; chainout = {40'b0, resulta_tmp}.

After: block A's input register reproduces ax_reg/ay_reg and block B's reproduces bx_reg/by_reg (same clock, same synchronous active-high clear driven by aclr, no enable in the original either). Block A's result register holds p0 = ax*ay exactly (16x8 unsigned, max 16711425 < 2^24, so res_a[23:0] is the exact product; same for p1), both cleared by aclr on the same edge that cleared resulta_tmp. cin_reg reproduces the one-cycle-later arrival of chainin at the old result register, so no assumption about chainin is needed; since the single instantiation ties it to 0, it constant-folds away in synthesis. resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg in 24-bit context equals (p0 + p1 + chainin) mod 2^24 on the same cycle as before, and chainout keeps its definition. Under clear, all three summands are 0, so resulta is 0, exactly as the cleared resulta_tmp. Every term of the original is kept; nothing inside a block is read elsewhere (the products were read only by that adder).

Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first aclr; the design holds aclr (reset_3, line 11343, from ~(i_valid || valid_11)) high while not valid, and the reference testbench asserts reset for 4 cycles and compares only after release, so nothing relies on an initial value.

Editor notes: delete the now-dead ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and their always block, keep the port list and the unused ena/mode as they are, and instantiate mult_add_int_18x19 by name only (do not add its definition to dla_like.small.v).

  1. Register stages, before and after (both converted regions)
  • Before: module port -> soft operand register (stage 1) -> hard multiply, 12-15-stage soft carry chain, one LUT -> resulta_tmp soft flip-flop with synchronous clear (stage 2) -> resulta. 2 register stages; the products are registered 0 stages before the sum (the table's "0 stage(s)").
  • After: module port -> block input register (stage 1) -> hard multiply inside the block -> block result register (stage 2) -> soft 24-bit adder, combinational -> resulta. Still 2 register stages, same latency, same cycle-by-cycle values; the products are now registered 1 stage before the sum, and the sum is combinational behind that register.
  1. Stages, and what leaves which stage
  • Leaving the slowest stage: the 24-bit soft adder chain and the final LUT leave the multiply's stage. The baseline stage was [soft operand FF -> wire 1.73 -> hard multiply -> wire 0.96 -> 12 carry stages -> wire 1.39 -> LUT -> resulta_tmp] = 6.81328 ns, the design's slowest; the second slowest, 6.808 ns, is the same shape in another instance and is converted too. The 48 soft operand flip-flops and the 24 resulta_tmp flip-flops per instance (72 x 72 = 5184 flip-flops in all) move into the blocks' registers and disappear from soft logic.
  • The multiply is now alone between the block's two registers: 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • The new longest stage the rewrite leaves is the one the adder moved into: block result register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire block into soft logic 1.376 (worst on this design; median 0.960) + 24-bit carry chain and LUT about 0.51 (what the baseline path leaves for its 12 carry stages plus LUT) + wire 1.39 to that LUT + setup 0.019 = 3.36 ns; with the median wire it is about 2.94 ns.
  • The stage feeding the blocks is register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire register to first block up to 2.461 (median 1.835) + input-register setup 0.019 = up to 2.54 ns, shorter than 3.36.
  • So the rewrite's own slowest stage is 3.36 ns against a 6.81328 ns baseline. The design's remaining stages are untouched and unlengthened; all of them are below the two converted paths, but the seed-1 report does not expose the third path, so the achieved period is max(3.36, slowest untouched stage).
  • Expected objective: tiles 114 -> 194 is +2.03e7 area, against about 1.45e7 recovered from the 5184 removed flip-flops, so area goes to roughly 1.73e8 (about +3.5%); delay drops by about half; wirelength is roughly flat to +15% (the same operand and result buses, no block-to-block routing, more spread over DSP columns). Product well below 1.04068e15.

DESIGN_CHANGE: inside dsp_block_16_8_false, registering the two products instead of the operands (ports straight into the multiplies, a product register, then the adder) would split the same 6.81 ns stage with no extra DSP tiles, at about 4.9 ns per stage (soft FF -> wire 1.73 -> multiply 2.14 -> wire 0.96 -> product FF); it keeps the module's 2-cycle latency and is a change to the design rather than a rewrite onto the block.
DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 carried its products at full width (and winograd_adder_16_20_4 summed them at that width) instead of truncating to the 16-bit resa_reg/resb_reg, and if the shared products were not re-read, negated and shifted outside their sums, the winograd trees could be folded into chains of these blocks.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

I'll read the design source now.

APPROVE

Checked against dla_like.small.v:

  • Module and readers (dla_like.small.v:11696-11752): ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg are read only at line 11745; resulta_tmp only at 11748-11749. Nothing the plan puts inside a block is visible elsewhere. The sole instantiation (11679-11690) ties ena=1'b1, chainin=64'd0, aclr=r_pipeline_0, matching the testbench's fixed values.
  • Timing/latency: original resulta_tmp <= ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin gives operands 2 cycles and chainin 1 cycle to resulta. The mapping reproduces both — block result registers carry the 2-cycle products, cin_reg carries the 1-cycle chainin — and resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg in 24-bit context equals the original's 64-bit sum truncated to 24. Products are exact (65535·255 < 2^24), so no wrap is lost.
  • Reset: aclr is a synchronous active-high clear of all five registers (11733-11739); driving the blocks' reset with it, plus the same clear on cin_reg, matches on the clear edge and on the first loading edge after release (both summands 0, resulta = chainin). Verified for 1-, 2-, 3-cycle and back-to-back pulses as the testbench applies them. reset_3 at 11343 is as cited.
  • Left regions: truncation to the 16-bit resa_reg/resb_reg before the 20-bit tree (8913, 8966-8967, 8879-8895), negative 16-bit coefficients (8928-8964), and multiple outside readers of dsp_out_* (9833, 9853, 9886 for dsp_out_1_5; dsp_out_1_4 in all four adders at 9813, 9843, 9872, 9902) all hold. All 16 winograd rows are accounted for.
  • Required items: blocks (144), tiles (194), longest block series (1, 2.219 ns vs 6.81328 ns), soft logic leaving the slowest stage (24-bit adder chain + LUT), and register stages before/after (2 → 2) are all stated. No ASSUMES is needed and none is claimed.

One citation slip, not affecting any decision: WA00 reads dsp_out_1_4 at line 9813 (data7x), not 9810 (data4x, which is dsp_out_1_2).

1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

prompt
Task

Edit dla_like.small.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167. The plan names their shared sum as 'resulta in dsp_block_16_8_false (the 24-bit sum ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin, line 11745)'. In the design their products are registered 0 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh dla_like.small.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

MAPPING: per dsp_block_16_8_false instance, two blocks, neither chained.
Block A (region $1166): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, ax[15:0]} (module port ax), ay={11'b0, ay[7:0]} (module port ay), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_a[63:0] (only res_a[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Block B (region $1167): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, bx[15:0]} (module port bx), ay={11'b0, by[7:0]} (module port by), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_b[63:0] (only res_b[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Soft logic kept in the module: cin_reg (24 bits, synchronous clear on aclr, else chainin[23:0]), assign resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg; (24-bit context, wraps mod 2^24), assign chainout = {40'b0, resulta};. ena stays unread, as in the original.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted and why

Both candidate products of dsp_block_16_8_false are converted, all 72 instances of each: $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166 (ax_reg*ay_reg) and $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167 (bx_reg*by_reg). They are the two products of the module's one sum (line 11745), and that sum's stage is the design's slowest (6.81328 ns) and second slowest (6.808 ns); the two reported paths are two different instances of the same shape, so the period only moves if every instance of the shape is fixed. 144 blocks, 2 per module instance.

The whole rewrite lives inside module dsp_block_16_8_false (lines 11696-11752). Its port list, every instantiation (line 11679 in dot_product_16_8_30_2) and every other module stay untouched; the reference testbench drives this module's ports (ena=1, chainin=0) and compares resulta and chainout every cycle, so the module's 2-cycle port-to-resulta latency and its aclr semantics must be preserved exactly, and they are (see 4).

  1. Candidates left, and why

All sixteen winograd rows are left: winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...:8966$733(sum_in=winograd_transform_0)#1..#4, the same four for winograd_transform_1, and the eight corresponding rows of $mul$...:8967$734. One reason covers all of them, and each of the three below is sufficient on its own:

  • Truncation. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16 bits (lines 8913, 8966-8967), so each product is reduced mod 2^16 before it enters the 20-bit adder tree in winograd_adder_16_20_4 (lines 8879-8895). A block carries the full 37-bit product into its sum, and sum(p_i mod 2^16) != (sum p_i) mod 2^20. Reproducing the per-product truncation requires the product outside the block, which defeats the conversion.
  • Visibility. Each dsp_out_* is read by several consumers outside any one sum: e.g. dsp_out_1_5 feeds winograd_adder_16_20_4_WA10.data0x (line 9833), is negated into f5 (line 9853) and shifted into f15 (line 9886); dsp_out_1_4 feeds WA00, WA10, WA20 and WA30 (lines 9810, 9843, 9872, 9902). A value read elsewhere cannot go inside a block.
  • Sign. coefa/coefb hold negative constants (-5, -10, -20, -25, lines 8928-8964) as 16-bit two's complement; the block zero-extends ay to 19 bits and its result is unsigned, so only the low 16 bits of the product would agree, and the tree adds 20-bit values. The same holds for the negated and arithmetically shifted operands.

Additionally, those sums mix the products with many non-product terms (shifted feature_reg_*) in a 4-level pipelined tree; a block chain would also change their latency.

  1. Blocks, tiles, and the longest series of blocks in a stage
  • Blocks added: 144 (2 per dsp_block_16_8_false instance x 72 instances). A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the converted logic occupies 144 tiles.
  • Baseline accounting: the design has 256 multiplies on DSPs (72 x 2 in dsp_block_16_8_false, 56 x 2 in winograd_dsp_16), i.e. 128 two_mult_18x19 blocks packed into 114 tiles (100 tiles with one block, 14 with two, = 256 multiplies). After the rewrite the 144 converted multiplies leave that pool and the 56 winograd blocks remain; at the baseline's packing density they take about 50 tiles. Total 194 tiles (worst case 200 if none of them pair), out of 306 on the device.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. Nothing is chained: chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block and every chainout is left unconnected, because a chained hop costs an extra register stage (block A's chainout is 1 cycle after its operands, and block B registers chainin at its entry, giving 3 cycles from port to result) while the module's contract is 2 cycles port-to-resulta. With no series of blocks, the block-to-block wire never appears; the block's own stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the 6.81328 ns baseline stage.
  1. Correctness

Original (line 11745, chainin tied to 0 at the only instantiation, line 11687): operand registers cleared by aclr, then resulta_tmp <= (ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin)[23:0], also cleared by aclr; chainout = {40'b0, resulta_tmp}.

After: block A's input register reproduces ax_reg/ay_reg and block B's reproduces bx_reg/by_reg (same clock, same synchronous active-high clear driven by aclr, no enable in the original either). Block A's result register holds p0 = ax*ay exactly (16x8 unsigned, max 16711425 < 2^24, so res_a[23:0] is the exact product; same for p1), both cleared by aclr on the same edge that cleared resulta_tmp. cin_reg reproduces the one-cycle-later arrival of chainin at the old result register, so no assumption about chainin is needed; since the single instantiation ties it to 0, it constant-folds away in synthesis. resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg in 24-bit context equals (p0 + p1 + chainin) mod 2^24 on the same cycle as before, and chainout keeps its definition. Under clear, all three summands are 0, so resulta is 0, exactly as the cleared resulta_tmp. Every term of the original is kept; nothing inside a block is read elsewhere (the products were read only by that adder).

Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first aclr; the design holds aclr (reset_3, line 11343, from ~(i_valid || valid_11)) high while not valid, and the reference testbench asserts reset for 4 cycles and compares only after release, so nothing relies on an initial value.

Editor notes: delete the now-dead ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and their always block, keep the port list and the unused ena/mode as they are, and instantiate mult_add_int_18x19 by name only (do not add its definition to dla_like.small.v).

  1. Register stages, before and after (both converted regions)
  • Before: module port -> soft operand register (stage 1) -> hard multiply, 12-15-stage soft carry chain, one LUT -> resulta_tmp soft flip-flop with synchronous clear (stage 2) -> resulta. 2 register stages; the products are registered 0 stages before the sum (the table's "0 stage(s)").
  • After: module port -> block input register (stage 1) -> hard multiply inside the block -> block result register (stage 2) -> soft 24-bit adder, combinational -> resulta. Still 2 register stages, same latency, same cycle-by-cycle values; the products are now registered 1 stage before the sum, and the sum is combinational behind that register.
  1. Stages, and what leaves which stage
  • Leaving the slowest stage: the 24-bit soft adder chain and the final LUT leave the multiply's stage. The baseline stage was [soft operand FF -> wire 1.73 -> hard multiply -> wire 0.96 -> 12 carry stages -> wire 1.39 -> LUT -> resulta_tmp] = 6.81328 ns, the design's slowest; the second slowest, 6.808 ns, is the same shape in another instance and is converted too. The 48 soft operand flip-flops and the 24 resulta_tmp flip-flops per instance (72 x 72 = 5184 flip-flops in all) move into the blocks' registers and disappear from soft logic.
  • The multiply is now alone between the block's two registers: 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • The new longest stage the rewrite leaves is the one the adder moved into: block result register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire block into soft logic 1.376 (worst on this design; median 0.960) + 24-bit carry chain and LUT about 0.51 (what the baseline path leaves for its 12 carry stages plus LUT) + wire 1.39 to that LUT + setup 0.019 = 3.36 ns; with the median wire it is about 2.94 ns.
  • The stage feeding the blocks is register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire register to first block up to 2.461 (median 1.835) + input-register setup 0.019 = up to 2.54 ns, shorter than 3.36.
  • So the rewrite's own slowest stage is 3.36 ns against a 6.81328 ns baseline. The design's remaining stages are untouched and unlengthened; all of them are below the two converted paths, but the seed-1 report does not expose the third path, so the achieved period is max(3.36, slowest untouched stage).
  • Expected objective: tiles 114 -> 194 is +2.03e7 area, against about 1.45e7 recovered from the 5184 removed flip-flops, so area goes to roughly 1.73e8 (about +3.5%); delay drops by about half; wirelength is roughly flat to +15% (the same operand and result buses, no block-to-block routing, more spread over DSP columns). Product well below 1.04068e15.
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).

13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The file dla_like.small.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: resulta in dsp_block_16_8_false (the 24-bit sum ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin, line 11745)
BLOCKS: 144
TILES: 194
STAGE_NS: 3.36
MAPPING: per dsp_block_16_8_false instance, two blocks, neither chained.
Block A (region $1166): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, ax[15:0]} (module port ax), ay={11'b0, ay[7:0]} (module port ay), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_a[63:0] (only res_a[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Block B (region $1167): clk=clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={2'b0, bx[15:0]} (module port bx), ay={11'b0, by[7:0]} (module port by), bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=res_b[63:0] (only res_b[23:0] read), chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.
Soft logic kept in the module: cin_reg (24 bits, synchronous clear on aclr, else chainin[23:0]), assign resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg; (24-bit context, wraps mod 2^24), assign chainout = {40'b0, resulta};. ena stays unread, as in the original.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted and why

Both candidate products of dsp_block_16_8_false are converted, all 72 instances of each: $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166 (ax_reg*ay_reg) and $mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167 (bx_reg*by_reg). They are the two products of the module's one sum (line 11745), and that sum's stage is the design's slowest (6.81328 ns) and second slowest (6.808 ns); the two reported paths are two different instances of the same shape, so the period only moves if every instance of the shape is fixed. 144 blocks, 2 per module instance.

The whole rewrite lives inside module dsp_block_16_8_false (lines 11696-11752). Its port list, every instantiation (line 11679 in dot_product_16_8_30_2) and every other module stay untouched; the reference testbench drives this module's ports (ena=1, chainin=0) and compares resulta and chainout every cycle, so the module's 2-cycle port-to-resulta latency and its aclr semantics must be preserved exactly, and they are (see 4).

  1. Candidates left, and why

All sixteen winograd rows are left: winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...:8966$733(sum_in=winograd_transform_0)#1..#4, the same four for winograd_transform_1, and the eight corresponding rows of $mul$...:8967$734. One reason covers all of them, and each of the three below is sufficient on its own:

  • Truncation. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16 bits (lines 8913, 8966-8967), so each product is reduced mod 2^16 before it enters the 20-bit adder tree in winograd_adder_16_20_4 (lines 8879-8895). A block carries the full 37-bit product into its sum, and sum(p_i mod 2^16) != (sum p_i) mod 2^20. Reproducing the per-product truncation requires the product outside the block, which defeats the conversion.
  • Visibility. Each dsp_out_* is read by several consumers outside any one sum: e.g. dsp_out_1_5 feeds winograd_adder_16_20_4_WA10.data0x (line 9833), is negated into f5 (line 9853) and shifted into f15 (line 9886); dsp_out_1_4 feeds WA00, WA10, WA20 and WA30 (lines 9810, 9843, 9872, 9902). A value read elsewhere cannot go inside a block.
  • Sign. coefa/coefb hold negative constants (-5, -10, -20, -25, lines 8928-8964) as 16-bit two's complement; the block zero-extends ay to 19 bits and its result is unsigned, so only the low 16 bits of the product would agree, and the tree adds 20-bit values. The same holds for the negated and arithmetically shifted operands.

Additionally, those sums mix the products with many non-product terms (shifted feature_reg_*) in a 4-level pipelined tree; a block chain would also change their latency.

  1. Blocks, tiles, and the longest series of blocks in a stage
  • Blocks added: 144 (2 per dsp_block_16_8_false instance x 72 instances). A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone, so the converted logic occupies 144 tiles.
  • Baseline accounting: the design has 256 multiplies on DSPs (72 x 2 in dsp_block_16_8_false, 56 x 2 in winograd_dsp_16), i.e. 128 two_mult_18x19 blocks packed into 114 tiles (100 tiles with one block, 14 with two, = 256 multiplies). After the rewrite the 144 converted multiplies leave that pool and the 56 winograd blocks remain; at the baseline's packing density they take about 50 tiles. Total 194 tiles (worst case 200 if none of them pair), out of 306 on the device.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. Nothing is chained: chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block and every chainout is left unconnected, because a chained hop costs an extra register stage (block A's chainout is 1 cycle after its operands, and block B registers chainin at its entry, giving 3 cycles from port to result) while the module's contract is 2 cycles port-to-resulta. With no series of blocks, the block-to-block wire never appears; the block's own stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, below the 6.81328 ns baseline stage.
  1. Correctness

Original (line 11745, chainin tied to 0 at the only instantiation, line 11687): operand registers cleared by aclr, then resulta_tmp <= (ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin)[23:0], also cleared by aclr; chainout = {40'b0, resulta_tmp}.

After: block A's input register reproduces ax_reg/ay_reg and block B's reproduces bx_reg/by_reg (same clock, same synchronous active-high clear driven by aclr, no enable in the original either). Block A's result register holds p0 = ax*ay exactly (16x8 unsigned, max 16711425 < 2^24, so res_a[23:0] is the exact product; same for p1), both cleared by aclr on the same edge that cleared resulta_tmp. cin_reg reproduces the one-cycle-later arrival of chainin at the old result register, so no assumption about chainin is needed; since the single instantiation ties it to 0, it constant-folds away in synthesis. resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg in 24-bit context equals (p0 + p1 + chainin) mod 2^24 on the same cycle as before, and chainout keeps its definition. Under clear, all three summands are 0, so resulta is 0, exactly as the cleared resulta_tmp. Every term of the original is kept; nothing inside a block is read elsewhere (the products were read only by that adder).

Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first aclr; the design holds aclr (reset_3, line 11343, from ~(i_valid || valid_11)) high while not valid, and the reference testbench asserts reset for 4 cycles and compares only after release, so nothing relies on an initial value.

Editor notes: delete the now-dead ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and their always block, keep the port list and the unused ena/mode as they are, and instantiate mult_add_int_18x19 by name only (do not add its definition to dla_like.small.v).

  1. Register stages, before and after (both converted regions)
  • Before: module port -> soft operand register (stage 1) -> hard multiply, 12-15-stage soft carry chain, one LUT -> resulta_tmp soft flip-flop with synchronous clear (stage 2) -> resulta. 2 register stages; the products are registered 0 stages before the sum (the table's "0 stage(s)").
  • After: module port -> block input register (stage 1) -> hard multiply inside the block -> block result register (stage 2) -> soft 24-bit adder, combinational -> resulta. Still 2 register stages, same latency, same cycle-by-cycle values; the products are now registered 1 stage before the sum, and the sum is combinational behind that register.
  1. Stages, and what leaves which stage
  • Leaving the slowest stage: the 24-bit soft adder chain and the final LUT leave the multiply's stage. The baseline stage was [soft operand FF -> wire 1.73 -> hard multiply -> wire 0.96 -> 12 carry stages -> wire 1.39 -> LUT -> resulta_tmp] = 6.81328 ns, the design's slowest; the second slowest, 6.808 ns, is the same shape in another instance and is converted too. The 48 soft operand flip-flops and the 24 resulta_tmp flip-flops per instance (72 x 72 = 5184 flip-flops in all) move into the blocks' registers and disappear from soft logic.
  • The multiply is now alone between the block's two registers: 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • The new longest stage the rewrite leaves is the one the adder moved into: block result register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire block into soft logic 1.376 (worst on this design; median 0.960) + 24-bit carry chain and LUT about 0.51 (what the baseline path leaves for its 12 carry stages plus LUT) + wire 1.39 to that LUT + setup 0.019 = 3.36 ns; with the median wire it is about 2.94 ns.
  • The stage feeding the blocks is register clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire register to first block up to 2.461 (median 1.835) + input-register setup 0.019 = up to 2.54 ns, shorter than 3.36.
  • So the rewrite's own slowest stage is 3.36 ns against a 6.81328 ns baseline. The design's remaining stages are untouched and unlengthened; all of them are below the two converted paths, but the seed-1 report does not expose the third path, so the achieved period is max(3.36, slowest untouched stage).
  • Expected objective: tiles 114 -> 194 is +2.03e7 area, against about 1.45e7 recovered from the 5184 removed flip-flops, so area goes to roughly 1.73e8 (about +3.5%); delay drops by about half; wirelength is roughly flat to +15% (the same operand and result buses, no block-to-block routing, more spread over DSP columns). Product well below 1.04068e15.

DESIGN_CHANGE: inside dsp_block_16_8_false, registering the two products instead of the operands (ports straight into the multiplies, a product register, then the adder) would split the same 6.81 ns stage with no extra DSP tiles, at about 4.9 ns per stage (soft FF -> wire 1.73 -> multiply 2.14 -> wire 0.96 -> product FF); it keeps the module's 2-cycle latency and is a change to the design rather than a rewrite onto the block.
DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 carried its products at full width (and winograd_adder_16_20_4 summed them at that width) instead of truncating to the 16-bit resa_reg/resb_reg, and if the shared products were not re-read, negated and shifted outside their sums, the winograd trees could be folded into chains of these blocks.

  • Scope/regions — only module dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11696-11771) changed; the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is identical, the port list and the sole instantiation (lines 11679-11690) are untouched, and the block's definition is not added to the file (only the two instantiations exist).
  • Mapping — two unchained blocks, exactly as planned: _a with ax={2'b0,ax}, ay={11'b0,ay} (11730-11742), _b with ax={2'b0,bx}, ay={11'b0,by} (11744-11756). All eight input ports connected on both (clk, reset=aclr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax, ay, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0), widths match the port widths; chainout/scanout left unconnected, so no block output goes anywhere the contract forbids.
  • Terms/arithmetic — 16×8 unsigned products max 16711425 < 2^24, so res_a[23:0]/res_b[23:0] are exact; resulta = res_a[23:0] + res_b[23:0] + cin_reg (11767) is a 24-bit context, equal to the original 64-bit sum truncated to 24 bits. chainout = {40'b0, resulta} reproduces the original definition.
  • Latency/registers — block operand regs + result reg give port→result in 2 cycles, matching the original operand-register→resulta_tmp path; cin_reg (11758-11766) reproduces the original 1-cycle chainin→resulta path, so no assumption about chainin is needed even though the testbench ties it to 0.
  • Reset — reset=aclr on both blocks and on cin_reg, synchronous active high: on a clearing edge all three summands are 0, so resulta=0 like the cleared resulta_tmp; on the first edge after release both blocks' operand regs were 0, so res_a=res_b=0 and resulta=chainin, matching the original. No reliance on power-up values; nothing was previously read from inside the blocks (ena and mode remain unread, as in the original).

Simulation evidence is consistent: 1134 cycles, 0 same-cycle mismatches on both outputs, 0 X on DUT only, with one mid-run reset pattern (sim_run.log:10-20). The "DELAYED 1 cy" count is a difference-from-previous-reference activity metric, not a leniency in the comparison (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v:83,86).
