ACCEPT

The edit matches the approved plan exactly and holds against the contract and correctness rules.

  • Plan conformance. Only lines 11727–11745 of dsp_block_16_8_false were replaced, with the text the plan specifies verbatim; mode (dla_like.small.v:11709-11710), the port list, and ena's non-use are untouched. sim_run.log:3 confirms nothing outside the module changed.
  • Ports. All eight block inputs are driven by name: clk, reset(aclr), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax({2'b0,ax}) 18b, ay({11'b0,ay}) 19b, bx({12'b0,prod_b}) 36b, chainin(64'b0), scanin(19'b0). result→res; chainout/scanout left unconnected, so the dedicated-chain and unused-scan rules hold.
  • Terms and widths. bx*by ≤ 65535·255 = 16711425 < 2^24, so the 24-bit prod_b is exact; the block's sum ≤ 2·16711425 is exact in 64 bits, and res[23:0] reproduces the original assignment's truncation to the 24-bit resulta_tmp. The + chainin term is dropped under the stated ASSUMES, supported by the sole instantiation at dla_like.small.v:11679 with .chainin(64'd0) (dla_like.small.v:11687) — grep confirms no other instantiation.
  • Registers/reset. The block model (sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v:42-51) is structurally isomorphic to the original always block with bx_reg holding bx*by instead of bx: both yield a(k+1)?0:(a(k)?0:(ax(k)·ay(k)+bx(k)·by(k))). reset is driven by the same synchronous active-high aclr, clearing operand and result registers together, so equivalence holds at every reset and release, not just start-up. Latency stays 2 cycles; no register added or moved across a port.
  • Visibility. prod_b and res are module-local; resulta and the module's chainout are both driven from the block's result register, not the block's chainout pin, preserving the original's registered 2-cycle value.
  • Simulation (sim_result.json, sim_run.log): 1134 cycles, 0 mismatches, 0 X on DUT, with chainin held 0 and ena held 1 as the design ties them.

12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
57

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain +0.80% against first, min-gain 0.0587 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac))
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 1.67592e+08 · critical path 6.81328 ns · wirelength 911396 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 1.04068e+15 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 5.87%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 5.87%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
